### Task 8 (P4): The signature canvas is a forced-light scope, and the scope sits on paper only (`src/components/asset-operations/signature-pad.tsx`)

Spec §4.4 ("the signature pad's canvas area … the PNG it exports must not change"), §6.2 (the theme lives only in `globals.css`; components never learn which theme is on). The scope goes on the canvas; its group stays themed (P-R4). The test also locks the four scope sites (P-R5).

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/components/asset-operations/signature-pad.tsx` (CRLF)
- Test: `tests/dark-mode-p.test.ts` (LF)

**Interfaces:** `LIGHT_SCOPES` (module const above this task's test): the four files with one literal `data-theme="light"` each.

**Changes at 87e4756:**

| Line | Now | Becomes | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| before `:117` (`<canvas`) | — | a JSX comment line at the indent of `<canvas`: `{/* The canvas is the forced-light scope, white paper on a dark page; the group around it follows the theme (dark mode P-R4). */}` | P-R4, P-R5 |
| `:118` | `ref={canvasRef}` | stays; a new line `data-theme="light"` after it, at its indent (the pin `tests/file-evidence-ui.test.ts:138` of the canvas class string holds) | P-R4 |

- [ ] **Step 1: The failing test.** Append to `tests/dark-mode-p.test.ts`:

```ts

// The forced-light scope sits on paper only (spec §4.4): the A4 sheet, the label sheet, the settings label sample and
// the signature canvas. No part forces dark; the attribute on <html> is the root layout's expression, not a literal.
const LIGHT_SCOPES: Record<string, number> = {
  [`${C}admin/system-settings-form.tsx`]: 1,
  [`${C}asset-operations/operation-document-print.tsx`]: 1,
  [`${C}asset-operations/signature-pad.tsx`]: 1,
  [`${C}assets/asset-label-print.tsx`]: 1,
}

test("the signature canvas is a forced-light scope, and the scope sits on paper only (dark mode P-R4, P-R5)", () => {
  const source = read(`${C}asset-operations/signature-pad.tsx`)
  assert.match(source, /<canvas\n\s+ref=\{canvasRef\}\n\s+data-theme="light"\n/)
  // The PNG it exports does not change: a white fill, the same stroke, cleared to white.
  assert.match(source, /context\.strokeStyle = "#0f172a"/)
  assert.equal(source.match(/context\.fillStyle = "#ffffff"/g)?.length, 2)
  assert.match(source, /onChange\(canvas\.toDataURL\("image\/png"\)\)/)
  // The group around it (its label, helper line and clear button) follows the theme.
  assert.match(source, /<div role="group" aria-labelledby=\{labelId\} aria-describedby=\{helperId\} className="rounded-md border border-border bg-background p-3">/)
  const counts = Object.fromEntries(
    sources.map((file) => [file.path, file.source.match(/\bdata-theme="light"/g)?.length ?? 0] as const).filter(([, count]) => count > 0),
  )
  assert.deepEqual(counts, LIGHT_SCOPES)
  // White paper always carries the scope, so tokens drawn on it stay light (the void banner's case, Review Focus 4). The
  // other solid whites keep their own colors: the camera controls and the preview's white page under a PDF (P-R8).
  const solidWhite = sources.filter((file) => /(?<![\w:-])bg-white(?![\w/-])/.test(file.source)).map((file) => file.path)
  assert.deepEqual(solidWhite.sort(), [
    `${C}asset-operations/operation-document-print.tsx`,
    `${C}asset-operations/signature-pad.tsx`,
    `${C}assets/asset-label-print.tsx`,
    `${C}audit/audit-scan-camera.tsx`,
    `${C}ui/attachment-preview-dialog.tsx`,
    `${C}ui/scanner-text-input.tsx`,
  ])
  // No part forces dark: the only literal is the dark block's selector in the CSS reader (Task 1, T1).
  assert.deepEqual(sources.filter((file) => /\bdata-theme="dark"/.test(file.source)).map((file) => file.path), ["src/lib/color-contrast.ts"])
})
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** per the Changes table.

```ts

// Every image of an uploaded file carries the image stage on its own element (spec §4.4, P-R1): a see-through PNG keeps
// its dark ink on a dark page, and a placeholder icon in the same frame keeps the frame's token fill. The images that
// are not uploads keep their own backing.
const NOT_UPLOADS: Record<string, number> = {
  "src/app/[locale]/(auth)/login/page.tsx": 1, // the app icon, framed in P7
  [`${C}asset-operations/operation-document-print.tsx`]: 1, // a signature on the white A4 paper
  [`${C}layout/sidebar.tsx`]: 1, // the app icon, framed in P7
  [`${C}ui/attachment-preview-dialog.tsx`]: 1, // the preview's black stage (spec §4.4)
}

test("every uploaded photo, logo and attachment image sits on the fixed light image stage (dark mode P-R1)", () => {
  const images = sources.flatMap((file) =>
    findJsxTags(file.source, ["img", "Image"]).map((tag) => ({ path: file.path, className: jsxAttrValue(tag.text, "className") ?? "" })),
  )
  assert.equal(images.length, 19, "every <img> and next/image in src")
  const unstaged: Record<string, number> = {}
  for (const image of images) {
    if (!/(?:^|\s)bg-image-stage(?:\s|$)/.test(image.className)) unstaged[image.path] = (unstaged[image.path] ?? 0) + 1
  }
  assert.deepEqual(unstaged, NOT_UPLOADS)
  // The stage is on the 15 images only, never on a frame that also shows a placeholder icon or a file type.
  assert.equal(sources.reduce((sum, file) => sum + (file.source.match(/\bbg-image-stage\b/g)?.length ?? 0), 0), 15)
  assert.match(read(`${C}assets/asset-thumbnail.tsx`), /"relative flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center overflow-hidden rounded-md border border-border bg-muted text-muted-foreground"/)
})
```

### Task 12 (S1): The theme cookie, its helpers and the chrome colors in one module (`src/lib/theme.ts`)

Spec §4.2, §4.3; fixed interface 1. A missing or unknown cookie means "follow the OS". `THEME_COLORS` is each theme's `--sidebar` (the sidebar, and after S5 the topbar and the bottom bar).

**Files:**
- Create: `src/lib/theme.ts` (LF, new)
- Create: `tests/dark-mode-s.test.ts` (LF, new; S2–S8 append to it)

**Interfaces:** the test header defines `read(path)` (LF). It imports `readDarkTokens` from T (see "Interfaces this area consumes").

**Changes at 87e4756:**

| Line | Now | Becomes | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| new file `src/lib/theme.ts` | — | the exports below; it imports nothing and has no `document.`, `window.`, `"use client"`, `from "@/` or unquoted `dark:` anywhere, comments included | S-R1 |

`src/lib/theme.ts` (LF, about 40 lines). Step 1's unit test fixes its behavior; these are the points it leaves to the writer:
- The five exports of the shared contract with their signatures, plus `export type ThemeColor = string | Array<{ media: string; color: string }>`, `export function themeColorFor(choice: ThemeChoice | null): ThemeColor` and `export function nextThemeChoice(current: string | undefined | null, prefersDark: boolean): ThemeChoice` (S-R1).
- `parseThemeChoice` accepts exactly `"light"` and `"dark"` (no trimming, no case folding); `themeCookieString` builds its string from `THEME_COOKIE`.
- `THEME_COLORS` is written exactly `export const THEME_COLORS = { "light": "#FFFFFF", "dark": "#0D182A" } as const` (quoted keys, S-R1; `#0D182A` is Task 1's dark `--sidebar`, R5 A-R6).
- `themeColorFor(null)` returns the light entry first: `[{ media: "(prefers-color-scheme: light)", color: THEME_COLORS.light }, { media: "(prefers-color-scheme: dark)", color: THEME_COLORS.dark }]`.
- A header comment says what the module is for (one cookie per browser; no cookie or another value follows the OS; the root layout reads it, `ThemeToggle` writes it; tests import it, so it has no `@/` import and no browser API). No comment may contain `document.`, `window.`, `"use client"`, `from "@/` or an unquoted `dark:` (the test and `tests/ui-overlay-guards.test.ts` read comments too).

- [ ] **Step 1: The failing test.** Create `tests/dark-mode-s.test.ts` (LF) with exactly:

```ts
import assert from "node:assert/strict"
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs"
import test from "node:test"
import { readDarkTokens, readRootTokens } from "../src/lib/color-contrast.ts"
import { THEME_COLORS, THEME_COOKIE, nextThemeChoice, parseThemeChoice, themeColorFor, themeCookieString } from "../src/lib/theme.ts"

// Dark mode area S (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-dark-mode-design.md §4, §5.2 "Top bars"): the theme cookie,
// the <html> attribute, theme-color, the toggle, the chrome color, the login headings, the 404 page and the offline page.
// One test per commit. Sources are read with LF line endings, so the regexes may contain \n.
const read = (path: string) => readFileSync(path, "utf8").replace(/\r\n/g, "\n")

test("the theme cookie holds light or dark for a year, and anything else follows the OS (dark mode spec §4.2, §4.3)", () => {
  assert.equal(THEME_COOKIE, "theme")
  assert.equal(parseThemeChoice("light"), "light")
  assert.equal(parseThemeChoice("dark"), "dark")
  for (const value of [undefined, null, "", "system", "Dark", " dark", "light;"]) assert.equal(parseThemeChoice(value), null, String(value))
  assert.equal(themeCookieString("dark"), "theme=dark; Path=/; Max-Age=31536000; SameSite=Lax")
  assert.equal(themeCookieString("light"), "theme=light; Path=/; Max-Age=31536000; SameSite=Lax")
  // The chrome color of each theme is that theme's --sidebar (the topbar and the phone bottom bar take it too).
  const css = read("src/app/globals.css")
  assert.equal(THEME_COLORS.light, readRootTokens(css).sidebar)
  assert.equal(THEME_COLORS.dark, readDarkTokens(css).sidebar)
  // theme-color: an explicit choice is one color; following the OS is the media pair.
  assert.equal(themeColorFor("light"), THEME_COLORS.light)
  assert.equal(themeColorFor("dark"), THEME_COLORS.dark)
  assert.deepEqual(themeColorFor(null), [
    { media: "(prefers-color-scheme: light)", color: THEME_COLORS.light },
    { media: "(prefers-color-scheme: dark)", color: THEME_COLORS.dark },
  ])
  // A press switches away from the theme on screen: the <html> attribute when it is set, else the OS.
  assert.equal(nextThemeChoice("light", true), "dark")
  assert.equal(nextThemeChoice("dark", false), "light")
  assert.equal(nextThemeChoice(undefined, true), "light")
  assert.equal(nextThemeChoice(undefined, false), "dark")
  assert.equal(nextThemeChoice("system", true), "light")
  // Plain helpers: tests import the module, and the server layout and the client toggle share it. No unquoted dark key
  // either: tests/ui-overlay-guards.test.ts bans the variant prefix in all of src, object keys included.
  assert.doesNotMatch(read("src/lib/theme.ts"), /from "@\/|"use client"|\bdocument\.|\bwindow\.|\bdark:/)
})
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `src/lib/theme.ts` as above.
- [ ] **Step 4: Guard edits:** none. `tests/ui-overlay-guards.test.ts` must stay green unchanged (quoted keys, S-R1).
- [ ] **Step 5: Verify.** `node --test tests/dark-mode-s.test.ts tests/ui-overlay-guards.test.ts tests/design-tokens-contrast.test.ts`; `npm test` (2216 tests, 2215 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped; +1, dry run); `npx tsc --noEmit`; `npm run lint` (0 errors, no new warning in `src/` or `tests/`). `node <tools>/eol.mjs src/lib/theme.ts tests/dark-mode-s.test.ts` prints `0` and its line count for `src/lib/theme.ts` (LF; 39 in the dry run), and `0 39`.

No cookie, or an unknown value, gives null: the page follows the OS.`, then `async function readThemeChoice() {` / `  return parseThemeChoice((await cookies()).get(THEME_COOKIE)?.value)` / `}` and a blank line, then the `viewport` export as it is | S-R2 |

- [ ] **Step 1: The failing test.** In `tests/dark-mode-s.test.ts`, add the line `import { findMatches, readSourceFiles } from "./helpers/source-files.ts"` directly below the `../src/lib/theme.ts` import (R5 A-R8), then append:

```ts

test("the root layout renders the cookie's choice on <html>, and no attribute while the page follows the OS (dark mode spec §4.2, §4.3)", () => {
  const layout = read("src/app/layout.tsx")
  assert.match(layout, /^import \{ cookies \} from "next\/headers"$/m)
  assert.match(layout, /^import \{ THEME_COOKIE, parseThemeChoice(?:, themeColorFor)? \} from "@\/lib\/theme"$/m)
  assert.match(layout, /\nasync function readThemeChoice\(\) \{\n  return parseThemeChoice\(\(await cookies\(\)\)\.get\(THEME_COOKIE\)\?\.value\)\n\}\n/)
  assert.match(layout, /const \[locale, themeChoice\] = await Promise\.all\(\[getLocale\(\), readThemeChoice\(\)\]\)/)
  // React leaves out an undefined attribute, so with no cookie <html> has no data-theme and the CSS follows the OS.
  assert.match(layout, /<html lang=\{locale\} data-theme=\{themeChoice \?\? undefined\} suppressHydrationWarning className=\{`\$\{plexSans\.variable\} \$\{thaiSans\.variable\} \$\{plexMono\.variable\}`\}>/)
  // No inline script and no theme package: the server attribute and the CSS media query decide the first paint.
  assert.doesNotMatch(layout, /<script|dangerouslySetInnerHTML|next-themes|ThemeProvider/)
  assert.equal(layout.match(/<html\b/g)?.length, 1)
  // Every page reads the choice (Review Focus 2): a route rendered at build time or from a cache would ship the HTML of no
  // choice, and the browser would follow the OS whatever the cookie says. Nothing in src/app outside the API opts out of
  // rendering per request, and Cache Components stays off.
  const pages = readSourceFiles("src/app").filter((file) => !file.path.startsWith("src/app/api/"))
  assert.deepEqual(findMatches(pages, /export const dynamic = "force-static"|\bgenerateStaticParams\b|export const revalidate\b|"use cache"/g), [])
  assert.doesNotMatch(read("next.config.ts"), /cacheComponents|dynamicIO/)
})
```

Spec §4.1, call 1; fixed interface 4; S-R4. The moon while the page is light ("switch to dark mode"), the sun while it is dark; a press switches at once and the cookie keeps the choice. The login page has none.

**Files:**

- Modify: `src/components/layout/topbar.tsx`, `src/app/globals.css`, `messages/th.json`, `messages/en.json` (all `i/lf w/crlf`)

|---|---|---|---|
| new file `theme-toggle.tsx` | — | the component described below | S-R4 |

`src/components/layout/theme-toggle.tsx` (LF). Step 1's test pins its `@/lib/theme` import, the `<Button>` line, the four face lines in order and the handler's three statements. What the test leaves open:
- `"use client"` first; imports `Moon` and `Sun` from `lucide-react`, `useTranslations` from `next-intl`, `Button` from `@/components/ui/button`, and `THEME_COLORS`, `nextThemeChoice`, `themeCookieString` from `@/lib/theme`.
- `export function ThemeToggle()` with `const t = useTranslations("common")` and `const switchTheme = () => { … }`, whose first line is `const root = document.documentElement`, then the pinned statements in order: `next` from `nextThemeChoice`; `root.dataset.theme` and `document.cookie`; a `for … of` over the `meta[name="theme-color"]` tags that sets `THEME_COLORS[next]` and removes `media`.
- The return is only the pinned `<Button>` around the four pinned faces. No state, no effect, no `aria-label`, no `title` (S-R4).
- One comment above the function says why both faces render (globals.css shows one, so nothing changes after hydration) and that a press switches at once and the cookie keeps the choice. No comment may contain a string the test forbids (`aria-label`, `title=`, `useState`, `useEffect`, `useSyncExternalStore`, `router.refresh`, an unquoted `dark:`) or a literal `data-theme="light"` or `data-theme="dark"` (P-R5).

The `globals.css` block: the three rules Step 1's test spells (the `faces` string, then the dark-OS and the light-OS `@media` strings), in that order, unlayered, two-space indents, with one comment above them: ThemeToggle renders both faces, and the theme on screen (the choice on `<html>`, else the OS) hides the other face, so the first paint is right with no script and nothing flips after hydration; unlayered so it beats the display utilities on the faces. The comment must not contain `color-scheme:` (Task 3 counts four outside `prefers-color-scheme:`), `forced-color-adjust` or `[data-theme="light"] {`.

    "en": { "common.switchToDark": "Switch to dark mode", "common.switchToLight": "Switch to light mode" }

- [ ] **Step 1: The failing test.** In `tests/dark-mode-s.test.ts`, add the line `const C = "src/components/"` directly below the `const read = …` line, then append:

```ts

test("the theme toggle sits beside the language button and shows the moon while light, the sun while dark, from the first paint (dark mode spec §4.1)", () => {
  const toggle = read(`${C}layout/theme-toggle.tsx`)
  assert.match(toggle, /^"use client"\n/)
  assert.match(toggle, /^export function ThemeToggle\(\) \{$/m)
  assert.match(toggle, /^import \{ THEME_COLORS, nextThemeChoice, themeCookieString \} from "@\/lib\/theme"$/m)
  // One ghost icon button, the size of the topbar's other icon buttons (44px below md, 36px from md). Both faces render
  // and globals.css hides one, so its name is the shown face's text and nothing is left to set after hydration.
  assert.equal(toggle.match(/<Button variant="ghost" size="icon" onClick=\{switchTheme\}>/g)?.length, 1)
  assert.match(toggle, /<Moon aria-hidden="true" data-when-theme="light" className="size-5" \/>\n\s+<Sun aria-hidden="true" data-when-theme="dark" className="size-5" \/>\n\s+<span data-when-theme="light" className="sr-only">\{t\("switchToDark"\)\}<\/span>\n\s+<span data-when-theme="dark" className="sr-only">\{t\("switchToLight"\)\}<\/span>/)
  assert.doesNotMatch(toggle, /aria-label|title=|useState|useEffect|useSyncExternalStore|router\.refresh|\bdark:/)
  // A press: the other theme from the one on screen goes on <html>, into the cookie and into theme-color, at once.
  assert.match(toggle, /const next = nextThemeChoice\(root\.dataset\.theme, window\.matchMedia\("\(prefers-color-scheme: dark\)"\)\.matches\)/)
  assert.match(toggle, /root\.dataset\.theme = next\n\s+document\.cookie = themeCookieString\(next\)/)
  assert.match(toggle, /document\.querySelectorAll<HTMLMetaElement>\('meta\[name="theme-color"\]'\)\) \{\n\s+meta\.content = THEME_COLORS\[next\]\n\s+meta\.removeAttribute\("media"\)/)
  // globals.css shows one face: the theme set on <html>, else the OS. Unlayered, so it beats the faces' utilities.
  const css = read("src/app/globals.css")
  const faces = '\n:root[data-theme="dark"] [data-when-theme="light"],\n:root[data-theme="light"] [data-when-theme="dark"] {\n  display: none;\n}\n'
  assert.ok(css.includes(faces), "the faces of an explicit choice")
  assert.ok(css.includes('\n@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {\n  :root:not([data-theme]) [data-when-theme="light"] {\n    display: none;\n  }\n}\n'), "the OS is dark")
  assert.ok(css.includes('\n@media (prefers-color-scheme: light) {\n  :root:not([data-theme]) [data-when-theme="dark"] {\n    display: none;\n  }\n}\n'), "the OS is light")
  const facesAt = css.indexOf(faces)
  const layerOpen = css.lastIndexOf("@layer", facesAt)
  assert.ok(layerOpen === -1 || css.indexOf("\n}", layerOpen) < facesAt, "the face rules sit outside every @layer")
  // In the topbar, directly after the language menu and before the user menu (the mockup's "TH" then the toggle).
  const topbar = read(`${C}layout/topbar.tsx`)
  assert.match(topbar, /^import \{ ThemeToggle \} from "@\/components\/layout\/theme-toggle"$/m)
  assert.match(topbar, /<span lang="en">English<\/span>[\s\S]*?<\/DropdownMenu>\n {8}<\/div>\n\n {8}<ThemeToggle \/>\n\n {8}\{\/\* User Menu \*\/\}/)
  assert.equal(topbar.match(/<ThemeToggle \/>/g)?.length, 1)
  // The login page has no topbar and no toggle: it follows the cookie or the OS.
  assert.doesNotMatch(read("src/app/[locale]/(auth)/login/page.tsx"), /ThemeToggle/)
  // Components never learn which theme is on (spec G6), and nothing but the toggle writes the cookie, so logging out keeps
  // the choice (spec §4.1, U5; Review Focus 3): only the theme module, the root layout and the toggle read the OS
  // setting, the <html> attribute or the cookie.
  const themeAware = ["src/lib/theme.ts", "src/app/layout.tsx", `${C}layout/theme-toggle.tsx`]
  assert.deepEqual(
    findMatches(readSourceFiles("src"), /prefers-color-scheme|dataset\.theme|\bTHEME_COOKIE\b|\bcookies\(\)|["'`]theme[="'`;]|from "@\/lib\/theme"/g, (path) => themeAware.includes(path)),
    [],
  )
  for (const [locale, toDark, toLight] of [["th", "เปลี่ยนเป็นโหมดมืด", "เปลี่ยนเป็นโหมดสว่าง"], ["en", "Switch to dark mode", "Switch to light mode"]]) {
    const common = JSON.parse(readFileSync(`messages/${locale}.json`, "utf8")).common
    assert.equal(common.switchToDark, toDark, locale)
    assert.equal(common.switchToLight, toLight, locale)
  }
})
```

| `mobile-field-navigation.tsx:51` | `className="fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 z-20 border-t border-border bg-surface px-2 pb-[max(0.5rem,env(safe-area-inset-bottom))] pt-1 lg:hidden"` | the same with `bg-sidebar` for `bg-surface` | S-R5 |

- [ ] **Step 1: The failing test.** In `tests/dark-mode-s.test.ts`, old `import { readDarkTokens, readRootTokens } from "../src/lib/color-contrast.ts"` → new `import { contrastRatio, parseHexColor, readDarkTokens, readRootTokens, resolveToken } from "../src/lib/color-contrast.ts"`, then append:

```ts

test("the topbar and the phone bottom bar take the sidebar's chrome color, the same white as today in light (dark mode spec §5.2 Top bars)", () => {
  const topbar = read(`${C}layout/topbar.tsx`)
  assert.match(topbar, /<header className="flex h-16 max-w-full shrink-0 items-center justify-between gap-1 border-b border-border bg-sidebar px-3 sm:gap-2 sm:px-4">/)
  const nav = read(`${C}layout/mobile-field-navigation.tsx`)
  assert.match(nav, /className="fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 z-20 border-t border-border bg-sidebar px-2 pb-\[max\(0\.5rem,env\(safe-area-inset-bottom\)\)\] pt-1 lg:hidden"/)
  for (const source of [topbar, nav]) assert.doesNotMatch(source, /\bbg-surface\b/)
  // The inks and the focus ring of both bars now sit on --sidebar, in both token sets.
  const css = read("src/app/globals.css")
  for (const [theme, tokens] of [["light", readRootTokens(css)], ["dark", readDarkTokens(css)]] as const) {
    const chrome = resolveToken(tokens, "sidebar")
    assert.equal(chrome, THEME_COLORS[theme], theme)
    for (const [ink, floor] of [["foreground", 4.5], ["muted-foreground", 4.5], ["primary", 4.5], ["ring", 3]] as const) {
      const ratio = contrastRatio(parseHexColor(resolveToken(tokens, ink)), parseHexColor(chrome))
      assert.ok(ratio >= floor, `${theme}: ${ink} on sidebar is ${ratio.toFixed(2)}:1`)
    }
  }
})
```

`src/app/not-found.tsx` (LF), a server component. Step 1's test pins its three imports exactly, `export default async function NotFound()`, the `Promise.all` line, the `<main>` line and the `ActionEmptyState` props in order. Not pinned, and written so: `ActionEmptyState` sits in `<div className="w-full max-w-xl">` inside the `<main>`; one comment above the function says that every unmatched URL and every `notFound()` lands here inside the root layout, so it follows the theme choice (Next's built-in page followed the OS only), and that the link goes to the locale home, which sends the person to login or to their own home.

test("the app's own 404 page follows the theme: tokens and the shared empty state inside the root layout (dark mode spec §4.5)", () => {
  const page = read("src/app/not-found.tsx")
  assert.doesNotMatch(page, /"use client"/)
  assert.match(page, /^export default async function NotFound\(\) \{$/m)
  assert.match(page, /const \[locale, t\] = await Promise\.all\(\[getLocale\(\), getTranslations\("common"\)\]\)/)
  assert.match(page, /<main className="flex min-h-dvh items-center justify-center bg-background px-4 py-8">/)
  assert.match(page, /<ActionEmptyState\n\s+icon=\{<SearchX \/>\}\n\s+title=\{t\("pageNotFoundTitle"\)\}\n\s+description=\{t\("pageNotFoundDescription"\)\}\n\s+actionHref=\{`\/\$\{locale\}`\}\n\s+actionLabel=\{t\("backToHome"\)\}\n\s+\/>/)
  assert.doesNotMatch(page, /<html|<body|style=|#[0-9A-Fa-f]{3,6}\b|\b(?:bg|text|border)-(?:white|black)\b/)
  // One 404 for the whole app, at the root: it catches unmatched URLs and every notFound(). No global-not-found (it
  // skips the root layout and its data-theme) and no global-error (it replaces the root layout, so it cannot read the choice).
  const special = readSourceFiles("src").map((file) => file.path).filter((path) => /\/(?:not-found|global-not-found|global-error)\.(?:tsx|ts|jsx|js)$/.test(path))
  assert.deepEqual(special, ["src/app/not-found.tsx"])
  assert.doesNotMatch(read("next.config.ts"), /globalNotFound/)
  // The root 404 renders outside src/app/[locale]/layout.tsx, so without next-intl's client provider: nothing it renders
  // may call a next-intl client hook (no test renders the page; Review Focus 5).
  assert.deepEqual(page.match(/^import .+$/gm), [
    'import { SearchX } from "lucide-react"',
    'import { getLocale, getTranslations } from "next-intl/server"',
    'import { ActionEmptyState } from "@/components/ui/action-empty-state"',
  ])
  for (const path of ["src/components/ui/action-empty-state.tsx", "src/components/ui/tone-icons.ts", "src/components/ui/button-variants.ts"]) {
    assert.doesNotMatch(read(path), /\buse(?:Translations|Locale|Messages|Formatter|Now|TimeZone)\(/, path)
  }
  const words = {
    th: ["ไม่พบหน้าที่ต้องการ", "ลิงก์อาจไม่ถูกต้อง หรือข้อมูลในหน้านี้ถูกลบหรือย้ายไปแล้ว", "กลับหน้าหลัก"],
    en: ["Page not found", "The link may be wrong, or what this page showed was removed or moved.", "Back to home"],
  }
  for (const [locale, [title, description, action]] of Object.entries(words)) {
    const common = JSON.parse(readFileSync(`messages/${locale}.json`, "utf8")).common
    assert.deepEqual([common.pageNotFoundTitle, common.pageNotFoundDescription, common.backToHome], [title, description, action], locale)
  }
})

- [ ] **Step 1: The failing test.** Append to `tests/dark-mode-s.test.ts` (write it with a file tool: it contains `\\` in a template literal, which a shell heredoc collapses — the rehearsal hit this):

```ts

test("the offline page follows the OS with the app's light and dark colors, and the service worker caches the new copy (dark mode spec §4.5)", () => {
  const offline = read("public/offline.html")
  // It cannot read the theme cookie without a script, so it follows the OS: color-scheme and theme-color for both.
  assert.match(offline, /<meta name="theme-color" media="\(prefers-color-scheme: light\)" content="#FFFFFF" \/>\n\s+<meta name="theme-color" media="\(prefers-color-scheme: dark\)" content="(#[0-9A-F]{6})" \/>/)
  assert.equal(offline.match(/<meta name="theme-color" media="\(prefers-color-scheme: dark\)" content="(#[0-9A-F]{6})"/)?.[1], THEME_COLORS.dark)
  assert.match(offline, /:root \{\n\s+color-scheme: light dark;/)
  assert.doesNotMatch(offline, /only light|<script/)
  const darkAt = offline.indexOf("@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {")
  assert.ok(darkAt > offline.indexOf("<style>") && darkAt < offline.indexOf("</style>"), "one dark block inside the page's style")
  const css = read("src/app/globals.css")
  const color = (block: string, selector: string, property: string) =>
    block.match(new RegExp(`(?:^|\\n)\\s*${selector} \\{[^}]*?(?:^|[\\s;{])${property}:[^;]*?(#[0-9a-f]{6});`))?.[1]?.toUpperCase()
  const schemes = [
    { theme: "light", block: offline.slice(0, darkAt), tokens: readRootTokens(css), border: "border", hint: "brand-navy" },
    { theme: "dark", block: offline.slice(darkAt), tokens: readDarkTokens(css), border: "border-color", hint: "foreground" },
  ] as const
  for (const { theme, block, tokens, border, hint } of schemes) {
    const expected: Array<[string, string, string]> = [
      [":root", "background", "canvas"],
      [":root", "color", "foreground"],
      ["main", border, "border"],
      ["main", "background", "card"],
      ["p", "color", "muted-foreground"],
      ["\\.hint", "background", "primary-soft"],
      ["\\.hint", "color", hint],
    ]
    for (const [selector, property, token] of expected) {
      assert.equal(color(block, selector, property), resolveToken(tokens, token), `${theme}: ${selector} ${property} is --${token}`)
    }
    const ratio = (ink: string, surface: string) => contrastRatio(parseHexColor(resolveToken(tokens, ink)), parseHexColor(resolveToken(tokens, surface)))
    for (const [ink, surface] of [["foreground", "canvas"], ["muted-foreground", "card"], [hint, "primary-soft"]]) {
      assert.ok(ratio(ink, surface) >= 4.5, `${theme}: ${ink} on ${surface}`)
    }
  }
  // A new cache name, so installed apps drop the light-only copy they precached.
  assert.match(read("public/sw.js"), /^const ASSET_SYSTEM_PWA_CACHE = "asset-system-pwa-v3"$/m)
})
```

Three implementer tasks, one commit each: `DESIGN.md` (D1), `docs/14_UI_UX_DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` with `.impeccable/design.json` (D2), then this plan's `DEVELOPER_HANDOFF.md` section and `docs/99_CHANGELOG.md` entry (D3). Task 23 also reads the findings file of the controller's Task 22, so it runs after Task 22 and before Task 24.

No task before Area D edits these five files or `tests/phase2d-docs.test.ts` (checked: `git diff 87e4756 9cf182c0 --stat -- DESIGN.md docs/14_UI_UX_DESIGN_SYSTEM.md .impeccable/design.json DEVELOPER_HANDOFF.md docs/99_CHANGELOG.md tests/phase2d-docs.test.ts` prints nothing in the dry run), so they hold when Tasks 20 and 21 run; every old text was checked to start on the stated line. **(M15)** :245, §2 Dark Theme (new): the dark set recorded where the light one is (spec §6.3), every #RRGGBB token of the dark block. Replace:

```
### Non-color values
```

with

```
### Dark Theme

Direction A, "navy dark" (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-dark-mode-design.md` §5): the light set's names in `:root[data-theme="dark"]` and its identical copy under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)`, each a 6-digit hex (the aliases `--surface`, `--destructive` and `--destructive-foreground` stay aliases). Navy-tinted darks, a light-blue primary, the icon's teal and lighter status inks on deep tinted fills.

- **Surfaces, darkest first:** `--canvas` #0A1424 · `--sidebar` #0D182A (the chrome: sidebar, topbar and phone bottom bar) · `--background` #0D192C (wells and dialog footers) · `--card` #101D31 (panels) · `--popover` #15243A (menus and popovers). Fills on a panel: `--muted` #1A2B44 and `--secondary` #1A2B44 (pressed), `--accent` #182A43 (hover), `--row-hover` #162741.
- **Inks:** `--foreground` #E4EBF5, `--card-foreground` #E4EBF5, `--popover-foreground` #E4EBF5, `--secondary-foreground` #E4EBF5, `--accent-foreground` #E4EBF5 · `--muted-foreground` #A9B7CC · `--faint-foreground` #8A9AB2 · `--neutral-ink` #BAC6D8.
- **Lines:** `--border-soft` #1A2A42 < `--border` #22344F < `--control-border` #34496A < `--input` #687DA0 · `--ring` #2FA9B1.
- **Brand and primary:** `--brand-navy` #5D93DE · `--brand-accent` #3CC6CE · `--primary` #6FA3EA · `--primary-hover` #8DB7F0 · `--primary-soft` #16294A · `--primary-border` #2F4F7E · `--primary-foreground` #08172C.
- **Status:** `--success` #5CCB7B, `--success-hover` #7DD796, `--success-soft` #10291B, `--success-border` #235C36, `--success-foreground` #08172C · `--warning` #F0A94D, `--warning-hover` #F4BF78, `--warning-soft` #2E2110, `--warning-border` #6B4A1C, `--warning-foreground` #08172C · `--danger` #F08A80, `--danger-hover` #F4A69E, `--danger-soft` #33171A, `--danger-border` #6E2E2B, `--danger-foreground` #08172C · `--info` #4FCBD3, `--info-hover` #7AD8DE, `--info-soft` #0E2A2E, `--info-border` #1F5B60, `--info-foreground` #08172C.
- **Sidebar:** `--sidebar-foreground` #D3DCE8 · `--sidebar-muted` #8E9DB4 · `--sidebar-hover` #16263D · `--sidebar-active` #1C3A66 · `--sidebar-active-foreground` #FFFFFF · `--sidebar-active-icon` #3CC6CE · `--sidebar-border` #22344F.
- **Images:** `--image-stage` #E9EEF5, the same in both themes: the fixed light stage under every uploaded photo, logo and attachment image (`bg-image-stage` on the image element, not on its frame, so see-through pixels and the side bands of a tall photo stay light while a placeholder icon follows the theme; in light it equals `--muted`, the old thumbnail fill). `--app-icon-frame` #34496A (light #FFFFFF): the 1px `ring-app-icon-frame` around the app icon in the sidebar and on the login page, a hairline that shows only in dark.

The rules of the dark set (spec §5.2):

- **Elevation by lightness:** canvas, then the chrome, the wells, the panels and the menus, each a step lighter; the overlay shadow is a deeper black (§4).
- **Solid fills take the dark ink:** primary and the status inks are lighter in dark, so their `-foreground` is #08172C, and `-hover` is lighter than the rest fill.
- **Brand navy is a fill only:** the pressed primary button (`active:bg-brand-navy`). It is never text in either theme; the login headings use `text-foreground`. The selected menu row stays navy with white text and the teal icon, so `--sidebar-active` equals `--brand-navy` only in light.
- **Tests:** `tests/design-tokens-contrast.test.ts` runs every rule of the light set on the dark set (test names start with `dark: `; a check against white uses the dark `--card`), and `tests/dark-mode-t.test.ts` checks that the two dark copies are identical, that every light token has a dark value and the reverse, and the elevation order.

### Non-color values
```

**(M23)** :268, §2 Named Rules: the Light Only Rule becomes the two-theme rule (spec §6.3). Replace:

```
**The Light Only Rule.** The app is light only: `:root { color-scheme: only light }` blocks Chrome Auto Dark and Samsung Internet dark mode. There is no `.dark` block, no `@custom-variant dark` and no `dark:` class (`tests/ui-overlay-guards.test.ts`, `tests/visual-foundation-guards.test.ts`).
```

with

```
**The Two-Theme Rule.** The app has a light and a dark theme from one set of token names (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-dark-mode-design.md`). The page follows the OS (`prefers-color-scheme`) until the person chooses: the toggle beside the language button in the topbar switches at once and writes the `theme` cookie (`light` or `dark`, one year, `Path=/`, `SameSite=Lax`, per browser; logging out keeps it), and the root layout renders the choice as `data-theme` on `<html>` (no attribute: follow the OS), so the first paint is right without a script. `src/lib/theme.ts` reads and writes the cookie and holds `THEME_COLORS`, the chrome color of each theme for `theme-color`, equal to `--sidebar`. The theme lives only in `src/app/globals.css`: the light `:root` block first, then `:root[data-theme="dark"]` and its identical copy under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)`, one forced-light scope `[data-theme="light"]` (it re-declares the light set and the light effects) and one `@media print` reset that makes every page light. Components use tokens only and never learn which theme is on: there is no `.dark` block, no `@custom-variant dark` and no `dark:` class (`tests/ui-overlay-guards.test.ts`, `tests/visual-foundation-guards.test.ts`). `color-scheme` is `light dark` on a root with no choice, `dark` for a dark choice and `only light` in the forced-light scope and in print, so native controls and scrollbars follow the theme and Chrome Auto Dark and Samsung Internet dark mode stay off; `accent-color` is `--primary`. Paper keeps its colors in both themes: the A4 documents with their VOID banner, the label sheet, its sample in Admin › Settings and the signature canvas carry `data-theme="light"` (exactly these four, `tests/dark-mode-p.test.ts`), the asset QR stays black on its white tile, the camera keeps its own colors (its N1 `EXEMPT` rows) and the attachment preview its black stage. Uploaded images sit on `--image-stage` and the app icon gets its `--app-icon-frame` hairline (Dark Theme above). The app's own 404 page (`src/app/not-found.tsx`) follows the theme, the offline page (`public/offline.html`) follows the OS, and the manifest splash and the iOS status bar stay light. `tests/design-tokens-contrast.test.ts` runs every contrast rule on both sets. Windows contrast themes (forced colors) override both themes: the forced-light scope sets no `forced-color-adjust`.
```

- [ ] **Step 1: The failing test.** Create `tests/dark-mode-docs.test.ts` (LF) with exactly this (write it with a file tool: it holds regex backslashes):

```ts
import assert from "node:assert/strict"
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs"
import test from "node:test"
import { readBlockTokens, readDarkTokens, readRootTokens } from "../src/lib/color-contrast.ts"

// Dark mode, area D (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-dark-mode-design.md §6.3): the design docs state the
// two-theme rule and record the dark set as src/app/globals.css declares it. "Light Only" stays out of the three
// canonical docs (DESIGN.md, docs/14, .impeccable/design.json); DEVELOPER_HANDOFF.md and docs/99_CHANGELOG.md are dated
// history. One test per docs commit; each fails before its commit. Sources are read with LF line endings.
const read = (path: string) => readFileSync(path, "utf8").replace(/\r\n/g, "\n")
const lightOnlyLines = (path: string) =>
  read(path)
    .split("\n")
    .flatMap((line, index) => (/\blight[ -]only\b/i.test(line) ? [`${path}:${index + 1}`] : []))
const globals = () => read("src/app/globals.css")
const isHex = (value: string) => /^#[0-9A-F]{6}$/.test(value)

test("DESIGN.md: the two-theme rule replaces Light Only, surfaces are named by role, and the dark set is as globals.css declares it (dark mode task 20, D1)", () => {
  assert.deepEqual(lightOnlyLines("DESIGN.md"), [])
  const design = read("DESIGN.md")
  assert.match(design, /\n\*\*The Two-Theme Rule\.\*\* The app has a light and a dark theme from one set of token names/)
  for (const fact of [
    /writes the `theme` cookie \(`light` or `dark`, one year, `Path=\/`, `SameSite=Lax`, per browser; logging out keeps it\)/,
    /renders the choice as `data-theme` on `<html>` \(no attribute: follow the OS\)/,
    /there is no `\.dark` block, no `@custom-variant dark` and no `dark:` class/,
    /one forced-light scope `\[data-theme="light"\]`/,
    /one `@media print` reset that makes every page light/,
    /`color-scheme` is `light dark` on a root with no choice, `dark` for a dark choice and `only light` in the forced-light scope and in print/,
    /carry `data-theme="light"` \(exactly these four, `tests\/dark-mode-p\.test\.ts`\)/,
    /`tests\/design-tokens-contrast\.test\.ts` runs every contrast rule on both sets/,
  ]) assert.match(design, fact)
  assert.match(design, /\n- \*\*Don't\*\* bring back a dark navy sidebar in the light theme /, "the navy sidebar Don't is scoped to the light theme")
  assert.match(design, /\n- \*\*Don't\*\* write a `dark:` class, a color for one theme or a component that reads the theme/)
  assert.match(design, /The theme toggle \(`ThemeToggle`, `src\/components\/layout\/theme-toggle\.tsx`/, "§5 Navigation: the toggle")
  // Surfaces are named by their role or their theme, not as white. White stays only where it is white in both themes
  // (the signature sheet, the selected row's text) or names the light theme.
  assert.doesNotMatch(design, /\bwhite (?:sidebar|topbar|panels?|card|frame|fill|header|box|body)\b|\bon white\b|Surface White|White-text|\*\*(?:Topbar|Sheets):\*\* white|\(white, |bodies are white|headers are white|`border`, white\b|\), white, /i)
  // §2 Dark Theme records every #RRGGBB token of the dark block once, with the value globals.css declares.
  const start = design.indexOf("\n### Dark Theme\n")
  assert.ok(start > design.indexOf("\n## 2. Colors\n") && start < design.indexOf("\n### Non-color values\n"), "Dark Theme sits in §2, before the non-color values")
  const section = design.slice(start, design.indexOf("\n### ", start + 1))
  const recorded: Record<string, string> = {}
  for (const [, name, value] of section.matchAll(/`--([a-z-]+)` (#[0-9A-F]{6})\b/g)) recorded[name] = value
  const css = globals()
  const dark = readDarkTokens(css)
  assert.deepEqual(recorded, Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(dark).filter(([, value]) => isHex(value))))
  // The two new tokens join the frontmatter with their light values, after the sidebar.
  const light = readRootTokens(css)
  assert.ok(design.includes(`\n  sidebar-border: "${light["sidebar-border"]}"\n  image-stage: "${light["image-stage"]}"\n  app-icon-frame: "${light["app-icon-frame"]}"\n`), "frontmatter: image-stage and app-icon-frame")
  // The effect values the text quotes are the ones of @theme (light) and of the dark block.
  const theme = readBlockTokens(css, "@theme")
  for (const name of ["color-scrim", "elevation-overlay", "halo-field-focus", "halo-field-error"]) {
    assert.ok(design.includes(`\`${theme[name]}\``), `the light --${name}`)
    assert.ok(design.includes(`\`${dark[name]}\``), `the dark --${name}`)
  }
  const stroke = (value: string) => `#${value.match(/stroke='%23([0-9A-Fa-f]{6})'/)?.[1]}`
  assert.ok(design.includes(`stroke ${stroke(theme["select-chevron-image"])} in \`@theme\` and ${stroke(dark["select-chevron-image"])} in the dark blocks`), "the chevron strokes")
})
```

  No existing assertion changes: `tests/phase2d-docs.test.ts` reads `DESIGN.md` for phrases these edits keep.

    }
    components: Array<{ name: string; description: string }>
    narrative: { rules: Array<{ name: string; body: string }>; dos: string[]; donts: string[] }
  }
  const design = JSON.parse(read(".impeccable/design.json")) as Design
  const css = globals()
  const light = readRootTokens(css)
  const dark = readDarkTokens(css)
  const theme = readBlockTokens(css, "@theme")
  // Each color records its light value as canonical and its dark value beside it, as the token blocks declare them.
  const TOKEN: Record<string, string> = { "soft-system-background": "background", "primary-slate-ink": "foreground" }
  for (const [key, entry] of Object.entries(design.extensions.colorMeta)) {
    const token = TOKEN[key] ?? key
    assert.deepEqual([key, entry.canonical, entry.dark], [key, light[token], dark[token]])
  }
  // Each shadow: its light value in @theme, its dark value in the dark block.
  const EFFECT: Record<string, string> = { "overlay": "elevation-overlay", "field-focus": "halo-field-focus", "field-error": "halo-field-error" }
  assert.deepEqual(design.extensions.shadows.map((shadow) => shadow.name).sort(), Object.keys(EFFECT).sort())
  for (const shadow of design.extensions.shadows) {
    const name = EFFECT[shadow.name]
    assert.deepEqual([shadow.name, shadow.value, shadow.darkValue], [shadow.name, theme[name], dark[name]])
  }
  const names = design.narrative.rules.map((rule) => rule.name)
  assert.equal(names.indexOf("The Two-Theme Rule"), names.indexOf("The Token Contrast Rule") + 1, "after The Token Contrast Rule, as in DESIGN.md §2")
  const rule = design.narrative.rules.find((entry) => entry.name === "The Two-Theme Rule")?.body ?? ""
  assert.match(rule, /Components use tokens only: no dark: class and no @custom-variant dark\./)
  assert.match(rule, /one forced-light \[data-theme="light"\] scope and one print reset/)
  assert.ok(rule.includes(`--image-stage ${light["image-stage"]} in both themes`) && light["image-stage"] === dark["image-stage"], "the image stage")
  assert.ok(rule.includes(`--app-icon-frame hairline, ${light["app-icon-frame"]} in light and ${dark["app-icon-frame"]} in dark`), "the app icon frame")
  assert.ok(rule.includes(`the dark ink ${dark["primary-foreground"]}`), "the dark ink of the solid fills")
  assert.ok(design.narrative.dos.some((entry) => entry.startsWith("Do keep navy #083161 for the logo and the selected menu row in the light theme,")))
  assert.ok(design.narrative.donts.some((entry) => entry.startsWith("Don't bring back a dark navy sidebar in the light theme ")))
  assert.ok(design.narrative.donts.some((entry) => entry.startsWith("Don't write a dark: class, a color for one theme or a component that reads the theme")))
  // Surfaces are named by role in the prose (the css examples keep the light values).
  const prose = [...design.components.map((entry) => entry.description), ...design.narrative.rules.map((entry) => entry.body)].join("\n")
  assert.doesNotMatch(prose, /\bwhite (?:sidebar|frame|header|body)\b|\bon white\b|White fill/)
})
```

## Dark Mode (<today>)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-dark-mode-design.md` (the user's decisions U1–U5) · plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-17-dark-mode.md` · canonical values in `DESIGN.md` (§2 Dark Theme and The Two-Theme Rule).

Messages (through `scripts/messages-edit.mjs`): 5 keys added, `common.switchToDark`, `common.switchToLight`, `common.pageNotFoundTitle`, `common.pageNotFoundDescription` and `common.backToHome`: 4062 → 4067 keys.
- **Behaviour:** the page follows the OS until the person chooses. The toggle beside the language button switches light ⇄ dark at once (a moon while light, a sun while dark, right from the first paint), and the `theme` cookie (`theme=<choice>; Path=/; Max-Age=31536000; SameSite=Lax`) keeps the choice in that browser, also after logging out. The root layout renders `data-theme` on `<html>` from the cookie (none: follow the OS) and `theme-color` (one color for a choice, the light/dark `media` pair without one), so the first paint is right with no script. The login page has no toggle and follows the cookie or the OS.
- **Tokens (Tasks 1–5):** the dark set, direction A "navy dark", in `:root[data-theme="dark"]` and its identical copy under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { :root:not([data-theme="light"]) }`; `tests/design-tokens-contrast.test.ts` runs every rule on both sets (20 → 36 tests). The scrim, the overlay shadow, the field halos and the select chevron are variables the dark blocks re-declare (`--elevation-overlay`, `--halo-field-*`, `--select-chevron-image`; T-R4, T-R5). One forced-light scope `[data-theme="light"]` and one print reset carry the light set and effects (T-R6, T-R7); `color-scheme` is `light dark`, `dark` or `only light` and `accent-color` is `--primary` (T-R8); every ring gap is `--card` (T-R9). New tokens: `--image-stage` (#E9EEF5 in both themes) and `--app-icon-frame` (#FFFFFF light, #34496A dark).

QR tiles, the camera, the preview stage and the server PDF are unchanged (P-R8).

The pins that encoded light only were rewritten in the commits that changed what they pin: VFG (the base and `color-scheme` pins, the ring gap, the `<html>` string, the bottom bar, the offline page and the cache name), `tests/modern-enterprise-theme.test.ts`, `tests/ui-overlay-guards.test.ts`, `tests/html-lang-and-years.test.ts`, `tests/visual-consistency-ui.test.ts`, `tests/dashboard-shell-theme.test.ts` and `tests/login-page-ui.test.ts`. ### โหมดมืด (Dark Mode)

Branch `feat/dark-mode` (จาก `master` `c0ad2aa`) · design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-dark-mode-design.md` · plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-17-dark-mode.md` · ค่าทั้งหมดอยู่ใน `DESIGN.md`

- **ขอบเขต:** เพิ่มชุดสีโหมดมืด (แนวทาง A สีกรมท่าเข้ม) คู่กับโหมดสว่าง · ไม่มีขั้นตอนงานใหม่ ไม่แตะฐานข้อมูล ไม่แก้ API ไม่เพิ่ม dependency · ข้อความ: เพิ่ม 5 คีย์ (ชื่อปุ่มสลับโหมด 2 คีย์ และหน้า 404 ของแอป 3 คีย์ ผ่าน `scripts/messages-edit.mjs`) · การตรวจกรอกไม่เปลี่ยน
- **การใช้งาน:** หน้าเว็บใช้โหมดตามเครื่องจนกว่าผู้ใช้เลือกเอง · ปุ่มข้างปุ่มภาษาบนแถบด้านบนสลับโหมดสว่างและโหมดมืดได้ทันที (รูปพระจันทร์ตอนสว่าง รูปพระอาทิตย์ตอนมืด) · คุกกี้ `theme` จำการเลือกไว้ในเบราว์เซอร์นั้น 1 ปี ออกจากระบบแล้วยังจำอยู่ · server ใส่ `data-theme` ที่ `<html>` และสีแถบของเบราว์เซอร์ (`theme-color`) มาตั้งแต่หน้าแรก หน้าจึงไม่กะพริบเป็นสีขาว · หน้าเข้าสู่ระบบไม่มีปุ่มสลับ ใช้ตามคุกกี้หรือตามเครื่อง
- **สี:** ค่าโหมดมืดทุกตัวผ่านกฎความต่างของสีชุดเดียวกับโหมดสว่าง (test ตรวจทั้งสองชุด) · ฉากหลังของ dialog เงา วงรอบช่องกรอกตอนโฟกัส และลูกศรของช่องเลือกเปลี่ยนตามโหมด · ช่องควบคุมของเบราว์เซอร์และแถบเลื่อนเปลี่ยนตามโหมด (`color-scheme`)
- **กระดาษและรูป:** เอกสาร A4 (รวมแถบ VOID) แผ่นป้าย ตัวอย่างป้ายในหน้าตั้งค่าระบบ และช่องลายเซ็นเป็นสีกระดาษเหมือนเดิมทั้งสองโหมด · พิมพ์ออกมาเป็นโหมดสว่างเสมอ · QR และภาพจากกล้องคงสีเดิม · รูปที่อัปโหลดและโลโก้อยู่บนพื้นสีอ่อนคงที่ · ไอคอนแอปมีเส้นกรอบบางในโหมดมืด · จุดตัวเลขบนกระดิ่งใช้สีตัวอักษรของ token
- **หน้าอื่น:** แถบด้านบนและแถบล่างบนมือถือใช้สีเดียวกับแถบด้านข้าง (โหมดสว่างยังเป็นสีขาว) · หัวข้อหน้าเข้าสู่ระบบใช้สีตัวอักษรหลัก · หน้า 404 ของแอปเองเปลี่ยนตามโหมด · หน้าออฟไลน์เปลี่ยนตามโหมดของเครื่อง
- **เอกสาร:** `DESIGN.md` ใช้กฎสองโหมดแทนกฎโหมดสว่างอย่างเดียว และบันทึกค่าโหมดมืดทุกตัว · `docs/14_UI_UX_DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` และ `.impeccable/design.json` ตาม (test ตรวจค่ากับ `globals.css`)
- **test:** ไฟล์ใหม่ `tests/dark-mode-t.test.ts` (4), `tests/dark-mode-p.test.ts` (7), `tests/dark-mode-s.test.ts` (8), `tests/dark-mode-docs.test.ts` (3) · `tests/design-tokens-contrast.test.ts` 20 → 36 · test 2188 → <N>
- **deploy:** ไม่ deploy · deploy ครั้งเดียวหลัง Dark Mode งาน V และชุด E2E ที่ทดสอบทั้งสองโหมด

## The user's answers (2026-10-17)

The four open issues went to the user before execution; every default stands:
- **T-a:** dialogs and sheets keep the panel color in dark; decide again from Task 22's screenshots (a later task if they blend into the page).
- **P-a:** the image stage is `#E9EEF5`.
- **S-a:** the 404 words as Task 18 writes them.
- **S-b:** no tooltip on the toggle.

The draft's open-issues text, kept for the record:

### Open issues as drafted

Each default is what the tasks write; an answer that differs changes the task named.

1. **T-a, dialogs and sheets in dark (the plan departs from spec §5.2 here).** The spec says "menus, popovers and dialogs are a step lighter than panels". Menus and popovers are (`--popover` `#15243A` on `--card` `#101D31`), but every dialog, alert dialog and sheet takes the panel color (`bg-surface` or `bg-card`; T-R11), so in dark they are `#101D31` on the 0.72 scrim. The other way is `bg-popover` on the three shells (`c/ui/accessible-dialog.tsx:84`, `c/ui/alert-dialog.tsx:61`, `c/ui/sheet.tsx:72`). Light would not change (both `#FFFFFF`), and every text pair passes on `#15243A` (status inks 6.43–8.04:1, primary 6.02, faint 5.46, ring 5.52, input 3.74). But a select menu opened inside a dialog would have the dialog's own color, every `bg-surface` field and box inside a dialog would become a darker box, and a skeleton there would drop to 1.09:1 (the floor is 1.15). It would also need a new task after Task 4, not rehearsed: the pin edits (a grep at `87e4756` finds `tests/confirm-dialog-ui.test.ts:49` and `tests/sheet-drawers-ui.test.ts:22`), `popover` added to the suite's status, primary, ring and neutral-ink lists, and new wording for DESIGN.md M8, M9, M33–M35 and design.json J23. **Recommendation: keep the panel color** (no change) and accept the deviation. Task 22 shows a dialog, an alert dialog and a sheet in dark with a menu open, so the choice can be revisited on screenshots.
2. **P-a, the image stage value.** `#E9EEF5` (default) keeps the register thumbnails, brand logos, my-assets and model photos exactly as today in light, and turns the asset page's three gallery photo buttons from `#F6F8FB` to `#E9EEF5`. `#F6F8FB` would do the reverse: the gallery stays, and the thumbnail photos sit on a lighter stage than their frames. The other value changes Task 1's three stage lines (Task 3 copies them), Task 5's `STAGE`, and the texts that quote it (M15, J28). **Recommendation: `#E9EEF5`**, because the register is the most-seen list.
3. **S-a, the 404 words.** th "ไม่พบหน้าที่ต้องการ" / "ลิงก์อาจไม่ถูกต้อง หรือข้อมูลในหน้านี้ถูกลบหรือย้ายไปแล้ว" / "กลับหน้าหลัก"; en "Page not found" / "The link may be wrong, or what this page showed was removed or moved." / "Back to home". A change edits Task 18's change file and the words in its test. **Recommendation: as written.**
4. **S-b, no tooltip on the toggle.** The spec does not ask for one. A `title` cannot follow the face that CSS shows without changing after hydration, so the toggle has none, like the language button; the bell and the sidebar toggle keep theirs. The other way is one neutral title for both states (for example th "โหมดสว่าง / โหมดมืด", en "Light / dark mode"): one more key and one prop in Task 15, and its test's `title=` ban changes. **Recommendation: no tooltip**; the accessible name already says what a press does.

**Light-theme changes beyond the spec's two** (the bars on `bg-sidebar`, the same `#FFFFFF`, and the login headings `#083161` → `#0B1D35`). A-R11 is the full list:
- the app's own 404 page replaces Next's built-in page (spec §4.5 asks for it; Task 18);
- the image stage (P-R1, open issue P-a): the asset page's three gallery photo buttons `#F6F8FB` → `#E9EEF5`; at nine other image sites only see-through pixels and the side bands of a tall photo turn `#E9EEF5` (Task 9);
- the login icon's 1px `#FFFFFF` ring on `#F6F8FB` (1.06:1, not visible; Task 11);
- `color-scheme: light dark` on a root with no choice (Task 3): nothing changes on a light OS, while a dark OS with no cookie now gets the dark theme and dark native controls (the spec's default, U2).

Nothing else changes in light: Review Focus 1 holds every light token value of `87e4756`, and Task 22 Step 5 compares every light screenshot with Task 0's.
